## Authentication Setup - Changelogue Project Analysis

Based on my thorough exploration of the ReleaseBeacon/Changelogue codebase, here's a complete overview of the current authentication architecture:

### 1. **Current Authentication Setup**

#### Backend (Go)
- **API Key Authentication** (`internal/api/auth.go`)
  - Bearer token-based authentication
  - Keys stored as SHA-256 hashes in the `api_keys` PostgreSQL table
  - Two key prefixes used: `rg_live_` (production) and `rg_test_` (development)
  - Middleware enforces authentication on all `/api/v1/*` routes except `/api/v1/health`
  - `NO_AUTH=true` environment variable disables auth entirely for development

#### Middleware Chain (`internal/api/middleware.go`)
Applied in order:
1. **RequestID** - Generates UUID, sets `X-Request-ID` header
2. **Logger** - Structured JSON logging with request ID, method, path, duration, status
3. **Recovery** - Panic recovery returning 500 with request ID
4. **RateLimit** - Token bucket per API key (10 rps, 20 burst) using `golang.org/x/time/rate`
5. **Auth** - Validates Bearer token via `KeyStore.ValidateKey()`

#### Frontend (Next.js/React)
- **No user/session management currently implemented**
- API client (`web/lib/api/client.ts`) makes unauthenticated requests (no Authorization header)
- All requests use `fetch()` with only `Content-Type: application/json` header
- Current development mode runs with `NO_AUTH=true` in `make run` command

### 2. **Database Schema**

#### API Keys Table
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS api_keys (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    key_hash VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL UNIQUE,           -- SHA-256 hash
    key_prefix VARCHAR(12) NOT NULL,                -- 'rg_live_' or 'rg_test_'
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    last_used_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);
```

**Current implementation:**
- `ValidateKey()` computes SHA-256 hash of raw key and queries by `key_hash`
- `TouchKeyUsage()` updates `last_used_at` asynchronously (fire-and-forget) on each request
- Keys are shown once at creation and hashed before storage

**NO user/session tables exist** — the system has no concept of user accounts, sessions, or OAuth.

### 3. **API Server Setup** (`cmd/server/main.go`)

```go
// Environment Variables
dbURL := envOr("DATABASE_URL", "postgres://localhost:5432/changelogue?sslmode=disable")
addr := envOr("LISTEN_ADDR", ":8080")
noAuth := os.Getenv("NO_AUTH") == "true"

// Dependencies structure includes KeyStore
type Dependencies struct {
    ...
    KeyStore      KeyStore
    NoAuth        bool
    ...
}

// Route registration conditionally applies auth
chain := publicChain
if !deps.NoAuth {
    chain = Chain(RequestID, Logger, Recovery, RateLimit(10, 20), Auth(deps.KeyStore))
}
```

### 4. **Frontend Architecture**

**Layout** (`web/app/layout.tsx`):
- Root layout with sidebar + main content area
- No auth guard or login page
- Metadata and font configuration only

**Page Example** (`web/app/page.tsx`):
- All pages are client components (`"use client"`)
- No authentication checking
- Direct API calls via `projects.list()` from `web/lib/api/client.ts`
- SSE connection to `/api/v1/events` for real-time updates (unauthenticated)

**API Client** (`web/lib/api/client.ts`):
```typescript
const BASE = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL || "/api/v1";

async function request<T>(path: string, init?: RequestInit): Promise<T> {
  const res = await fetch(`${BASE}${path}`, {
    headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json", ...init?.headers },
    ...init,
  });
  // No Authorization header added
}
```

### 5. **Environment Variables**

**README.md defines:**
| Variable | Default | Purpose |
|----------|---------|---------|
| `DATABASE_URL` | `postgres://localhost:5432/changelogue?sslmode=disable` | PostgreSQL connection |
| `LISTEN_ADDR` | `:8080` | HTTP server bind address |
| `NO_AUTH` | _(unset)_ | Set to `true` to disable API key auth (development) |
| `LOG_LEVEL` | `info` | Log level |
| `LLM_PROVIDER` | `gemini` | LLM provider |
| `GOOGLE_API_KEY` / `OPENAI_API_KEY` | _(empty)_ | LLM keys |

**Development workflow:**
```bash
make run    # Runs: NO_AUTH=true ./changelogue
make dev    # Runs: docker compose up -d && make run
```

### 6. **Architecture Decision Notes** (from ARCH.md & DESIGN.md)

- **API Key Auth:** Bearer token authentication with hashed key storage
- **NO_AUTH mode:** Development-only bypass (line 39-41 in `cmd/server/main.go`)
- **Rate Limiting:** Per-key token bucket (10 rps, 20 burst) with `X-Ratelimit-*` response headers
- **Public endpoints:** Only `/api/v1/health` is public (for load balancer health checks)
- **CORS:** Applied at server level allowing all origins

### 7. **Key Findings for GitHub Login Feature**

**Current State:**
- ✅ Bearer token authentication framework exists
- ✅ PostgreSQL backing with key hashing (SHA-256)
- ✅ Rate limiting infrastructure in place
- ❌ **No user accounts table**
- ❌ **No session management**
- ❌ **No OAuth/GitHub integration**
- ❌ **Frontend has no login flow or auth context**
- ❌ **API client doesn't support Authorization header injection**
- ❌ **No protected routes on frontend**

**Middleware Stack** is production-ready and extensible:
- File: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/middleware.go`
- File: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/auth.go`
- Uses stdlib `func(http.Handler) http.Handler` pattern

**Database Foundation:**
- File: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/db/migrations.go`
- Migrations run idempotently at startup via River's `rivermigrate`
- Can add user/session tables easily

**Route Registration:**
- File: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/server.go` (lines 34-43)
- `RegisterRoutes()` function conditionally applies auth chain based on `NoAuth` flag
- All routes except `/health` and frontend static files are protected

**Frontend API Integration:**
- File: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/lib/api/client.ts` (line 31-42)
- Single request function can be enhanced to inject Authorization headers
- All API calls are routed through this function

## Proposed Approaches

### Approach A: Standard OAuth 2.0 Flow (Recommended)

**Flow**: Browser → `/auth/github` → GitHub authorize → callback → create session → redirect to dashboard

- Go backend handles the entire OAuth2 flow (authorization URL, token exchange, GitHub API call for user info)
- Session stored in PostgreSQL `sessions` table, browser gets an `HttpOnly` secure cookie
- Allowlist configured via env vars: `ALLOWED_GITHUB_USERS=alice,bob` and `ALLOWED_GITHUB_ORGS=mycompany`
- Frontend wraps layout in an auth check — unauthenticated users see a login page
- Existing API key auth continues to work for machine-to-machine access; both auth methods coexist

**Pros**: Simple, battle-tested, no extra dependencies. Everything stays in Go stdlib + `net/http`.
**Cons**: Slightly more backend code than using a library.

### Approach B: Use an OAuth Library (e.g., `goth`)

Same flow but uses the `goth` library to abstract GitHub OAuth.

**Pros**: Less boilerplate for the OAuth dance.
**Cons**: Extra dependency, less control, the OAuth flow is ~50 lines of code anyway.

### Approach C: NextAuth.js on the Frontend

Move OAuth handling to the Next.js layer using NextAuth.js.

**Pros**: Rich ecosystem, handles session/token automatically.
**Cons**: Requires Next.js server mode (you currently use static export + `embed`). Would break the single-binary deployment model.

---

**My recommendation: Approach A.** It fits your current architecture (static Next.js export embedded in Go binary), adds no new dependencies, and keeps auth logic centralized in the Go backend where your existing API key auth already lives.

Does Approach A work for you, or would you prefer a different direction?

> DEVELOPER

A

Now let me present the design in sections.

---

## Design Section 1: OAuth Flow & Backend Routes

**New routes** (all under `/auth/`, outside the existing API key middleware):

| Route | Purpose |
|-------|---------|
| `GET /auth/github` | Redirect to GitHub's OAuth authorize URL with `state` param |
| `GET /auth/github/callback` | Receive code from GitHub, exchange for token, fetch user info, check allowlist, create session, set cookie, redirect to `/` |
| `GET /auth/me` | Return current user info (from session cookie) — used by frontend |
| `POST /auth/logout` | Delete session, clear cookie |

**OAuth flow**:
1. Frontend redirects to `/auth/github`
2. Backend generates a random `state`, stores it temporarily (in-memory map with TTL), redirects to `https://github.com/login/oauth/authorize?client_id=...&state=...&scope=read:org`
3. GitHub redirects back to `/auth/github/callback?code=...&state=...`
4. Backend validates `state`, exchanges `code` for access token via `https://github.com/login/oauth/access_token`
5. Fetches `https://api.github.com/user` and `https://api.github.com/user/orgs` with the token
6. Checks username against `ALLOWED_GITHUB_USERS` and org memberships against `ALLOWED_GITHUB_ORGS`
7. If allowed: upsert user row, create session, set `HttpOnly` cookie, redirect to `/`
8. If denied: redirect to `/login?error=unauthorized`

**Env vars**:
- `GITHUB_CLIENT_ID` — GitHub OAuth App client ID
- `GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET` — GitHub OAuth App client secret
- `ALLOWED_GITHUB_USERS` — comma-separated GitHub usernames (e.g. `alice,bob`)
- `ALLOWED_GITHUB_ORGS` — comma-separated GitHub org logins (e.g. `mycompany,myteam`)
- `SESSION_SECRET` — secret key for signing session cookies

Does this section look right?

> DEVELOPER

Ok

> AGENT

## Design Section 2: Database Schema

**Two new tables** added to the existing migration system in `internal/db/migrations.go`:

**`users` table:**
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (
    id              UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    github_id       BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    github_login    VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    name            VARCHAR(200),
    avatar_url      TEXT,
    created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
);
```

**`sessions` table:**
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sessions (
    id          UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    user_id     UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    expires_at  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
    created_at  TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
);
CREATE INDEX idx_sessions_user_id ON sessions(user_id);
CREATE INDEX idx_sessions_expires_at ON sessions(expires_at);
```

**Key decisions:**
- User is upserted on each login (updates `github_login`, `name`, `avatar_url`, `updated_at`)
- Sessions expire after 7 days (configurable). Expired sessions cleaned up periodically.
- Session ID is stored in an `HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax; Path=/` cookie named `session`
- Deleting a user cascades to their sessions

Does this look right?

> DEVELOPER

I'm considering extend it to a finegrained user registeration, login by email / google/ github and access control by orgnizations, can this approach be extended to that in the future?

> AGENT

Yes, absolutely. This design extends naturally to that:

**Email/password login**: Add `email`, `password_hash` columns to `users`. The `github_id` is already nullable-friendly (you'd make it `UNIQUE` but nullable). Add `/auth/login` and `/auth/register` routes that create sessions the same way.

**Google OAuth**: Same OAuth2 flow — add `google_id` column to `users`, add `/auth/google` and `/auth/google/callback` routes. The session/cookie mechanism is identical.

**Multiple providers per user**: When you need account linking, add a `user_identities` table:
```
user_identities(user_id, provider, provider_id, email)
```
and keep `users` as the canonical account. But that's a future migration — no need to build it now.

**Org-based access control**: The `users` table is the anchor. You'd add an `organizations` table and `org_members(org_id, user_id, role)` join table. The current GitHub org allowlist check is a simple gate — it can evolve into proper org membership without changing the session or auth flow.

**What stays the same regardless of provider:**
- Session table and cookie mechanism — unchanged
- `/auth/me` and `/auth/logout` — unchanged
- Frontend auth context — unchanged
- API key auth for machines — unchanged

The only things that grow are: columns on `users`, new `/auth/<provider>` route pairs, and eventually a `user_identities` table if you want account linking.

So yes — we can keep this design simple for now (GitHub only) and it won't paint you into a corner. Want me to continue with the next design section?

> DEVELOPER

Sure

> AGENT

## Design Section 3: Backend Auth Package

**New package**: `internal/auth/` with these components:

| File | Responsibility |
|------|----------------|
| `oauth.go` | GitHub OAuth flow: authorize redirect, callback handler, token exchange, user info fetch |
| `session.go` | Session CRUD: create, validate, delete, cleanup expired |
| `middleware.go` | `RequireSession` middleware — checks cookie, loads user, injects into `context.Context` |
| `allowlist.go` | Parse env vars, check username/org membership |

**Middleware integration**: The existing API routes keep their API key auth. The new `RequireSession` middleware is applied to page-serving routes and `/auth/me`. Both auth methods coexist:

```
/auth/*          → no auth (public OAuth endpoints)
/api/v1/health   → no auth (health check)
/api/v1/*        → API key auth (existing, for machine access)
/auth/me         → session auth (cookie)
/auth/logout     → session auth (cookie)
```

**Frontend API calls**: The dashboard currently calls `/api/v1/*` endpoints. In dev mode (`NO_AUTH=true`), these continue to work without auth. In production, the frontend will need to either:
- Use session cookies for API calls too (add session auth as a fallback in the existing API auth middleware), or
- Keep API key auth separate and only use sessions for page access

**My recommendation**: Extend the existing API auth middleware to accept either a valid Bearer token OR a valid session cookie. This way the frontend works seamlessly with cookies, and machine clients still use API keys.

Does this approach work?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

## Design Section 4: Frontend Changes

**New files/changes in `web/`:**

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `web/app/login/page.tsx` | Login page with "Sign in with GitHub" button |
| `web/lib/auth/context.tsx` | `AuthProvider` + `useAuth()` hook — fetches `/auth/me` on mount, provides user state |
| `web/app/layout.tsx` | Wrap app in `AuthProvider`, redirect to `/login` if unauthenticated |

**Login page**: Minimal — app logo, "Sign in with GitHub" button that navigates to `/auth/github`. Shows error message if redirected with `?error=unauthorized`.

**Auth context**:
```
AuthProvider
  → on mount: fetch /auth/me
  → if 200: set user, render children
  → if 401: redirect to /login
  → loading state while checking
```

**Logout**: Add a user avatar + dropdown to the sidebar with a "Sign out" option that POSTs to `/auth/logout` and redirects to `/login`.

**No changes to API client** (`web/lib/api/client.ts`): Since we're using `HttpOnly` cookies with `SameSite=Lax`, the browser automatically sends them with same-origin requests. The existing `fetch()` calls just work — no Authorization header needed for session-based auth.

Does this look right?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

## Design Section 5: Development & NO_AUTH Compatibility

**Dev mode behavior**: When `NO_AUTH=true`:
- API key auth is already bypassed (existing behavior)
- Session auth middleware also bypasses — injects a fake dev user into context
- `/auth/me` returns a hardcoded dev user (no GitHub credentials needed)
- Login page still accessible but not required
- No `GITHUB_CLIENT_ID` / `GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET` needed in dev

**Session cleanup**: A goroutine that runs every hour, deletes sessions where `expires_at < NOW()`. Starts alongside the existing polling engine in `cmd/server/main.go`.

**Cookie settings**:
- Dev: `HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax; Path=/` (no `Secure` flag, works on `localhost`)
- Prod: `HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax; Path=/`
- Determined by checking if `LISTEN_ADDR` is localhost or if a `SECURE_COOKIES=true` env var is set

**State parameter storage**: In-memory map with 10-minute TTL. Sufficient for a single-instance deployment. If multi-instance is needed later, move to DB.

Does this all make sense?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

{
  "file_path": "/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-16-github-oauth-login-design.md",
  "content": "# GitHub OAuth Login Design\n\n## Overview\n\nAdd GitHub OAuth login to Changelogue with server-side sessions, restricted to an allowlist of GitHub usernames and/or org memberships. Existing API key auth for machine clients is preserved — both auth methods coexist.\n\n## OAuth Flow\n\n### Routes\n\n| Route | Auth | Purpose |\n|-------|------|---------|\n| `GET /auth/github` | None | Redirect to GitHub OAuth authorize URL |\n| `GET /auth/github/callback` | None | Receive code, exchange for token, validate user, create session |\n| `GET /auth/me` | Session | Return current user info |\n| `POST /auth/logout` | Session | Delete session, clear cookie |\n\n### Flow\n\n1. Frontend redirects to `/auth/github`\n2. Backend generates random `state`, stores in memory (10-min TTL), redirects to `https://github.com/login/oauth/authorize?client_id=...&state=...&scope=read:org`\n3. GitHub redirects to `/auth/github/callback?code=...&state=...`\n4. Backend validates `state`, exchanges `code` for access token via `https://github.com/login/oauth/access_token`\n5. Fetches `https://api.github.com/user` and `https://api.github.com/user/orgs`\n6. Checks username against `ALLOWED_GITHUB_USERS` and org logins against `ALLOWED_GITHUB_ORGS`\n7. If allowed: upsert user, create session, set HttpOnly cookie, redirect to `/`\n8. If denied: redirect to `/login?error=unauthorized`\n\n### Environment Variables\n\n| Variable | Required | Purpose |\n|----------|----------|---------|\n| `GITHUB_CLIENT_ID` | Prod | GitHub OAuth App client ID |\n| `GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET` | Prod | GitHub OAuth App client secret |\n| `ALLOWED_GITHUB_USERS` | No | Comma-separated GitHub usernames |\n| `ALLOWED_GITHUB_ORGS` | No | Comma-separated GitHub org logins |\n| `SESSION_SECRET` | Prod | Secret for signing session cookies |\n| `SECURE_COOKIES` | No | Set `true` to enable Secure flag on cookies |\n\nAt least one of `ALLOWED_GITHUB_USERS` or `ALLOWED_GITHUB_ORGS` must be set in production.\n\n## Database Schema\n\nTwo new tables added to `internal/db/migrations.go`:\n\n```sql\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (\n    id              UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n    github_id       BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE,\n    github_login    VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,\n    name            VARCHAR(200),\n    avatar_url      TEXT,\n    created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),\n    updated_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()\n);\n\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sessions (\n    id          UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n    user_id     UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,\n    expires_at  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,\n    created_at  TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()\n);\nCREATE INDEX idx_sessions_user_id ON sessions(user_id);\nCREATE INDEX idx_sessions_expires_at ON sessions(expires_at);\n```\n\n- Users are upserted on each login (updates login, name, avatar_url, updated_at)\n- Sessions expire after 7 days\n- Deleting a user cascades to their sessions\n\n### Future extensibility\n\n- Email/password: add `email`, `password_hash` columns to `users`\n- Google OAuth: add `google_id` column, new `/auth/google` routes\n- Multi-provider linking: add `user_identities(user_id, provider, provider_id, email)` table\n- Org-based access control: add `organizations` and `org_members` tables\n- None of these require changing the session or cookie mechanism\n\n## Backend Package: `internal/auth/`\n\n| File | Responsibility |\n|------|----------------|\n| `oauth.go` | GitHub OAuth: authorize redirect, callback, token exchange, user info fetch |\n| `session.go` | Session CRUD: create, validate, delete, cleanup expired |\n| `middleware.go` | `RequireSession` middleware: check cookie, load user, inject into context |\n| `allowlist.go` | Parse env vars, check username/org membership |\n\n### Auth middleware integration\n\nThe existing API auth middleware (`internal/api/auth.go`) is extended to accept either:\n- A valid Bearer API key (existing behavior), OR\n- A valid session cookie\n\nThis allows the frontend to use cookie-based auth for `/api/v1/*` calls seamlessly, while machine clients continue using API keys.\n\nRoute auth mapping:\n\n```\n/auth/github          → no auth (public)\n/auth/github/callback → no auth (public)\n/auth/me              → session cookie\n/auth/logout          → session cookie\n/api/v1/health        → no auth (existing)\n/api/v1/*             → API key OR session cookie\n```\n\n## Frontend Changes\n\n### New files\n\n| File | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `web/app/login/page.tsx` | Login page: app logo, \"Sign in with GitHub\" button, error display |\n| `web/lib/auth/context.tsx` | `AuthProvider` + `useAuth()` hook |\n\n### Auth context\n\n```\nAuthProvider\n  → on mount: fetch /auth/me\n  → if 200: set user state, render children\n  → if 401: redirect to /login\n  → shows loading state while checking\n```\n\n### Layout changes (`web/app/layout.tsx`)\n\n- Wrap app in `AuthProvider`\n- Unauthenticated users are redirected to `/login`\n\n### Sidebar changes\n\n- Add user avatar + dropdown with \"Sign out\" option\n- Sign out POSTs to `/auth/logout`, redirects to `/login`\n\n### No changes to API client\n\n`web/lib/api/client.ts` uses `fetch()` which automatically sends `HttpOnly` cookies with same-origin requests. No Authorization header needed for session-based auth.\n\n## Development & NO_AUTH Mode\n\nWhen `NO_AUTH=true`:\n- API key auth bypass continues (existing)\n- Session auth middleware bypasses — injects a fake dev user\n- `/auth/me` returns a hardcoded dev user\n- No `GITHUB_CLIENT_ID` / `GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET` needed\n- Login page accessible but not required\n\n## Cookie Configuration\n\n| Setting | Dev | Prod |\n|---------|-----|------|\n| HttpOnly | Yes | Yes |\n| Secure | No | Yes (when `SECURE_COOKIES=true`) |\n| SameSite | Lax | Lax |\n| Path | `/` | `/` |\n| Name | `session` | `session` |\n\n## Session Cleanup\n\nA background goroutine runs hourly to delete expired sessions (`expires_at < NOW()`). Starts alongside the existing polling engine in `cmd/server/main.go`.\n\n## State Parameter Storage\n\nOAuth `state` values stored in an in-memory map with 10-minute TTL. Sufficient for single-instance deployment. Can be moved to PostgreSQL if multi-instance is needed later.\n"
}
```

Now I have a thorough understanding of the codebase and the spec. Here is my complete review.

---

## Spec Review: GitHub OAuth Login Design

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-16-github-oauth-login-design.md`

**Verdict: ISSUES FOUND**

---

### BLOCKING Issues

**1. BLOCKING -- Both allowlists empty permits universal access**

The spec says:
> "At least one of `ALLOWED_GITHUB_USERS` or `ALLOWED_GITHUB_ORGS` must be set in production."

But it does not specify what happens if neither is set. There is no enforcement mechanism described -- no startup validation, no fallback behavior. If neither env var is set and no check is performed, the allowlist check would pass vacuously (checking against two empty lists), granting access to **any** GitHub user. The spec must explicitly state:
- The server MUST refuse to start (or refuse to enable OAuth routes) if both lists are empty and `NO_AUTH` is not `true`.
- This should be a startup validation in `main.go`, consistent with how the existing codebase handles missing config (exits with `slog.Error` + `os.Exit(1)`).

**2. BLOCKING -- GitHub access token stored nowhere, but used to fetch orgs**

The flow says:
1. Exchange code for access token (step 4)
2. Fetch `/user` and `/user/orgs` (step 5)
3. Create session (step 7)

But the GitHub access token is never stored and never mentioned again. This has two problems:
- If org membership needs to be re-validated on session refresh or periodic checks, there is no token to do so.
- The spec should explicitly state that the token is **discarded after initial validation** and document that this is intentional (org membership is checked at login time only, not continuously).

**3. BLOCKING -- No CSRF protection on `/auth/logout`**

The spec defines `POST /auth/logout` as session-authenticated, but does not mention CSRF protection. With `SameSite=Lax` cookies, POST requests from cross-origin forms will include the session cookie (browsers only block cross-site cookies on top-level GETs with `SameSite=Lax` for navigations; the spec on `Lax` actually blocks cross-site POSTs). However, this is a subtle area frequently misunderstood. The spec should explicitly call out that:
- `SameSite=Lax` blocks cross-site POST requests from sending cookies, providing CSRF protection for the logout endpoint.
- OR add an explicit CSRF token mechanism.

After re-checking: `SameSite=Lax` does indeed block cookies on cross-site POSTs. Downgrading this to SUGGESTION -- but the spec should document why CSRF tokens are not needed (the `SameSite=Lax` reasoning), because an implementer otherwise may not understand the security rationale.

**Revised severity: SUGGESTION** (see below)

**4. BLOCKING -- Auth middleware integration is underspecified**

The spec says the existing `Auth` middleware in `internal/api/auth.go` should be "extended to accept either a Bearer API key OR a valid session cookie." Looking at the actual code:

```go
func Auth(store KeyStore) Middleware {
    return func(next http.Handler) http.Handler {
        return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
            header := r.Header.Get("Authorization")
            if !strings.HasPrefix(header, "Bearer ") {
                RespondError(w, r, http.StatusUnauthorized, "unauthorized", "Missing API key")
                return
            }
            ...
        })
    }
}
```

The current middleware hard-fails if there is no Bearer header. The spec does not describe:
- The precedence: check Bearer first, then cookie? Or cookie first?
- What `KeyStore` interface method is needed for session validation (a new interface is implied but not named).
- Whether `RequireSession` middleware (mentioned in `internal/auth/middleware.go`) is separate from the modified `Auth` middleware, or the same. The spec lists both `RequireSession` (in `internal/auth/`) and says `Auth` is "extended" (in `internal/api/auth.go`). This is contradictory -- which middleware handles `/api/v1/*` requests?
- What user context key is used. The existing `Auth` does not inject any user into context. The spec should specify the context key for the authenticated user so that API handlers can access the user identity.

**5. BLOCKING -- `SESSION_SECRET` purpose is ambiguous**

The spec describes `SESSION_SECRET` as "Secret for signing session cookies" but the session ID is a random UUID stored in PostgreSQL. If the cookie value is just the session UUID, what is being signed? Options:
- (a) The cookie value is `HMAC(session_id, SESSION_SECRET)` -- prevents session ID guessing but the session ID is already a UUID (122 bits of entropy).
- (b) The cookie is a signed cookie using `SESSION_SECRET` to prevent tampering (e.g., `session_id.signature`).

The spec must clarify the cookie format. If it is just a raw UUID, `SESSION_SECRET` is unnecessary. If it is signed, the verification logic should be described.

**6. BLOCKING -- Frontend login redirect creates infinite loop risk with static export**

The spec says:
```
AuthProvider
  -> on mount: fetch /auth/me
  -> if 401: redirect to /login
```

And `layout.tsx` wraps the entire app in `AuthProvider`. But `/login` is also within the app router and would be wrapped by `AuthProvider`. This means:
- User lands on `/login` -> `AuthProvider` fires -> fetches `/auth/me` -> gets 401 -> redirects to `/login` -> infinite loop.

The spec must either:
- Exclude `/login` from `AuthProvider` protection (e.g., check `pathname` before redirecting).
- Use a separate layout for the login page that does not include `AuthProvider`.

**7. BLOCKING -- `dynamicRoutes` in `frontend.go` needs `/login` entry**

The existing `RegisterFrontend` in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/frontend.go` serves static pages. For the new `/login` page to work, either:
- Next.js static export produces `login.html` and the fallback to `path + ".html"` handles it (this would work), OR
- A `dynamicRoutes` entry is needed.

The first case should work, but the spec should explicitly confirm this. More critically, the spec does not address: what happens when the Go backend receives a request to `/auth/github` or `/auth/github/callback`? These are new Go-handled routes that must be registered **before** the catch-all `GET /` frontend handler. The spec should mention route registration order, since `http.ServeMux` uses longest-prefix matching and `GET /auth/github` would correctly take precedence over `GET /`.

**Revised severity: SUGGESTION** -- `ServeMux` pattern matching handles this correctly, but worth documenting.

---

### SUGGESTION Issues

**8. SUGGESTION -- State parameter in-memory map has no size bound**

The spec says OAuth `state` values are stored "in an in-memory map with 10-minute TTL." But there is no mention of:
- Maximum map size / rate limiting on `/auth/github` to prevent memory exhaustion from repeated anonymous requests.
- Cleanup mechanism (is there a goroutine sweeping expired entries, or are they cleaned on access?).

An attacker could flood `/auth/github` with requests to grow the map unboundedly.

**9. SUGGESTION -- Session cleanup goroutine lifecycle not specified**

The spec says "A background goroutine runs hourly to delete expired sessions." The existing pattern in `main.go` uses `go orch.Run(ctx)` with context cancellation. The spec should clarify:
- The goroutine respects the context for clean shutdown.
- Whether it uses a `time.Ticker` or `time.After` pattern.

This is minor but should be consistent with the existing orchestrator pattern.

**10. SUGGESTION -- Missing `github_login` uniqueness consideration**

The `users` table has `github_id BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE` but `github_login VARCHAR(100)` is not unique. GitHub allows username changes, so the same `github_id` could have different `github_login` values over time. This is correct. However, the allowlist check is against `ALLOWED_GITHUB_USERS` which matches usernames. If a user changes their GitHub username after being added to the allowlist, they lose access. The spec should document this behavior explicitly.

**11. SUGGESTION -- No `read:org` scope limitation documented**

The spec requests `scope=read:org` which grants read access to org membership. This is correct for checking org membership. However, if `ALLOWED_GITHUB_ORGS` is empty (only user allowlist is used), the `read:org` scope is unnecessary. Consider documenting that the scope is always requested for simplicity, or conditionally including it.

**12. SUGGESTION -- Schema migration uses CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS but spec uses CREATE TABLE**

The existing migration pattern in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/db/migrations.go` uses `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` and `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`. The spec's SQL uses `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` for tables but `CREATE INDEX` (without `IF NOT EXISTS`) for the session indexes. This would fail on re-run. The spec should use `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` to match the existing pattern.

**13. SUGGESTION -- No session sliding window / refresh mechanism**

Sessions expire after a fixed 7 days. There is no mention of:
- Sliding expiry (extending the session on each authenticated request).
- What the user experience is when a session expires mid-use (they get a 401 on the next API call and the frontend redirects to login -- this should be documented explicitly).

**14. SUGGESTION -- CSRF rationale should be documented**

As noted above, `SameSite=Lax` provides CSRF protection for non-GET mutations. The spec should include a brief security note explaining why a CSRF token is not needed, so implementers do not add one unnecessarily or, worse, assume it is an oversight.

**15. SUGGESTION -- `SECURE_COOKIES` default should be documented**

The spec shows `SECURE_COOKIES` defaults to not-set (no Secure flag). It would be safer to default to `true` and require `SECURE_COOKIES=false` explicitly for local dev. This follows the principle of secure-by-default.

**16. SUGGESTION -- The `/auth/me` and `/auth/logout` routes are under `/auth/` but the existing API is under `/api/v1/`**

This is an inconsistency in URL structure. The existing codebase routes everything through `/api/v1/`. Placing auth routes under `/auth/` works but breaks the pattern. The spec should justify this choice (e.g., "OAuth callback URLs should be short for GitHub App configuration") or move these to `/api/v1/auth/`.

**17. SUGGESTION -- No mention of logging / audit trail for login events**

The existing codebase logs at `slog.Info` and `slog.Warn` levels. The spec should specify what is logged on:
- Successful login (user, IP, timestamp).
- Failed login (denied user, reason).
- Session creation / deletion.

This is important for security auditing.

**18. SUGGESTION -- `users` table has no relationship to existing domain entities**

The `users` table is standalone -- no FK to `projects`, `api_keys`, or any other entity. This is fine for the initial implementation, but the spec's "future extensibility" section mentions org-based access control. Consider whether a `created_by` field on projects or an `api_keys.user_id` column should be mentioned in the future extensibility section.

---

### Summary

| # | Issue | Severity |
|---|-------|----------|
| 1 | Both allowlists empty permits universal access -- no startup validation described | BLOCKING |
| 2 | GitHub access token lifecycle not documented (stored? discarded?) | BLOCKING |
| 3 | Auth middleware integration underspecified (precedence, interfaces, context key) | BLOCKING |
| 4 | `SESSION_SECRET` purpose ambiguous -- what cookie format is signed? | BLOCKING |
| 5 | Login page infinite redirect loop -- `AuthProvider` wraps `/login` | BLOCKING |
| 6 | State map unbounded -- DoS via `/auth/github` flooding | SUGGESTION |
| 7 | Session cleanup goroutine lifecycle not specified | SUGGESTION |
| 8 | Username change breaks allowlist access (document this) | SUGGESTION |
| 9 | `read:org` scope always requested even when unnecessary | SUGGESTION |
| 10 | `CREATE INDEX` without `IF NOT EXISTS` breaks idempotent migrations | SUGGESTION |
| 11 | No session sliding window or expiry UX documented | SUGGESTION |
| 12 | CSRF protection rationale should be documented | SUGGESTION |
| 13 | `SECURE_COOKIES` should default to true (secure-by-default) | SUGGESTION |
| 14 | `/auth/` route prefix inconsistent with `/api/v1/` pattern | SUGGESTION |
| 15 | No login/logout audit logging specified | SUGGESTION |
| 16 | Users table has no relationship to domain entities | SUGGESTION |
| 17 | Frontend route registration order should be documented | SUGGESTION |

The spec is well-structured and covers the major design decisions. The five BLOCKING issues should be resolved before implementation begins, as they would either cause security vulnerabilities (issues 1, 4), runtime bugs (issue 5), or leave implementers guessing at critical details (issues 2, 3).

    47→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (
    48→    id              UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    49→    github_id       BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    50→    github_login    VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    51→    name            VARCHAR(200),
    52→    avatar_url      TEXT,
    53→    created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    54→    updated_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
    55→);
    56→
    57→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sessions (
    58→    id          UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    59→    user_id     UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    60→    expires_at  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
    61→    created_at  TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
    62→);
    63→CREATE INDEX idx_sessions_user_id ON sessions(user_id);
    64→CREATE INDEX idx_sessions_expires_at ON sessions(expires_at);
    65→```
    66→
    67→- Users are upserted on each login (updates login, name, avatar_url, updated_at)
    68→- Sessions expire after 7 days
    69→- Deleting a user cascades to their sessions
    70→
    71→### Future extensibility
    72→
    73→- Email/password: add `email`, `password_hash` columns to `users`
    74→- Google OAuth: add `google_id` column, new `/auth/google` routes
    75→- Multi-provider linking: add `user_identities(user_id, provider, provider_id, email)` table
    76→- Org-based access control: add `organizations` and `org_members` tables
    77→- None of these require changing the session or cookie mechanism
    78→
    79→## Backend Package: `internal/auth/`
    80→
    81→| File | Responsibility |
    82→|------|----------------|
    83→| `oauth.go` | GitHub OAuth: authorize redirect, callback, token exchange, user info fetch |
    84→| `session.go` | Session CRUD: create, validate, delete, cleanup expired |
    85→| `middleware.go` | `RequireSession` middleware: check cookie, load user, inject into context |
    86→| `allowlist.go` | Parse env vars, check username/org membership |
    87→
    88→### Auth middleware integration
    89→
    90→The existing API auth middleware (`internal/api/auth.go`) is extended to accept either:
    91→- A valid Bearer API key (existing behavior), OR
    92→- A valid session cookie
    93→
    94→This allows the frontend to use cookie-based auth for `/api/v1/*` calls seamlessly, while machine clients continue using API keys.
    95→
    96→Route auth mapping:
    97→
    98→```
    99→/auth/github          → no auth (public)
   100→/auth/github/callback → no auth (public)
   101→/auth/me              → session cookie
   102→/auth/logout          → session cookie
   103→/api/v1/health        → no auth (existing)
   104→/api/v1/*             → API key OR session cookie
   105→```
   106→
   107→## Frontend Changes
   108→
   109→### New files
   110→
   111→| File | Purpose |
   112→|------|---------|
   113→| `web/app/login/page.tsx` | Login page: app logo, "Sign in with GitHub" button, error display |
   114→| `web/lib/auth/context.tsx` | `AuthProvider` + `useAuth()` hook |
   115→
   116→### Auth context
   117→
   118→```
   119→AuthProvider
   120→  → on mount: fetch /auth/me
   121→  → if 200: set user state, render children
   122→  → if 401: redirect to /login
   123→  → shows loading state while checking
   124→```
   125→
   126→### Layout changes (`web/app/layout.tsx`)
   127→
   128→- Wrap app in `AuthProvider`
   129→- Unauthenticated users are redirected to `/login`
   130→
   131→### Sidebar changes
   132→
   133→- Add user avatar + dropdown with "Sign out" option
   134→- Sign out POSTs to `/auth/logout`, redirects to `/login`
   135→
   136→### No changes to API client
   137→
   138→`web/lib/api/client.ts` uses `fetch()` which automatically sends `HttpOnly` cookies with same-origin requests. No Authorization header needed for session-based auth.
   139→
   140→## Development & NO_AUTH Mode
   141→
   142→When `NO_AUTH=true`:
   143→- API key auth bypass continues (existing)
   144→- Session auth middleware bypasses — injects a fake dev user
   145→- `/auth/me` returns a hardcoded dev user
   146→- No `GITHUB_CLIENT_ID` / `GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET` needed
   147→- Login page accessible but not required
   148→
   149→## Cookie Configuration
   150→
   151→| Setting | Dev | Prod |
   152→|---------|-----|------|
   153→| HttpOnly | Yes | Yes |
   154→| Secure | No | Yes (when `SECURE_COOKIES=true`) |
   155→| SameSite | Lax | Lax |
   156→| Path | `/` | `/` |
   157→| Name | `session` | `session` |
   158→
   159→## Session Cleanup
   160→
   161→A background goroutine runs hourly to delete expired sessions (`expires_at < NOW()`). Starts alongside the existing polling engine in `cmd/server/main.go`.
   162→
   163→## State Parameter Storage
   164→
   165→OAuth `state` values stored in an in-memory map with 10-minute TTL. Sufficient for single-instance deployment. Can be moved to PostgreSQL if multi-instance is needed later.
   166→

{
  "file_path": "/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-16-github-oauth-login-design.md",
  "content": "# GitHub OAuth Login Design\n\n## Overview\n\nAdd GitHub OAuth login to Changelogue with server-side sessions, restricted to an allowlist of GitHub usernames and/or org memberships. Existing API key auth for machine clients is preserved — both auth methods coexist.\n\n## OAuth Flow\n\n### Routes\n\n| Route | Auth | Purpose |\n|-------|------|---------|\n| `GET /auth/github` | None | Redirect to GitHub OAuth authorize URL |\n| `GET /auth/github/callback` | None | Receive code, exchange for token, validate user, create session |\n| `GET /auth/me` | Session | Return current user info |\n| `POST /auth/logout` | Session | Delete session, clear cookie |\n\n### Flow\n\n1. Frontend redirects to `/auth/github`\n2. Backend generates random `state`, stores in memory (10-min TTL, max 1000 entries), redirects to `https://github.com/login/oauth/authorize?client_id=...&state=...&scope=read:org`\n3. GitHub redirects to `/auth/github/callback?code=...&state=...`\n4. Backend validates `state`, exchanges `code` for access token via `https://github.com/login/oauth/access_token`\n5. Fetches `https://api.github.com/user` and `https://api.github.com/user/orgs` using the access token\n6. **Access token is discarded** after fetching user info — it is not stored. Org membership is checked at login time only.\n7. Checks username against `ALLOWED_GITHUB_USERS` and org logins against `ALLOWED_GITHUB_ORGS`\n8. If allowed: upsert user, create session, set HttpOnly cookie, redirect to `/`\n9. If denied: redirect to `/login?error=unauthorized`\n\n**Logging**: Log successful logins (`slog.Info`) and denied logins (`slog.Warn`) with username and reason.\n\n### Environment Variables\n\n| Variable | Required | Purpose |\n|----------|----------|---------|\n| `GITHUB_CLIENT_ID` | Prod | GitHub OAuth App client ID |\n| `GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET` | Prod | GitHub OAuth App client secret |\n| `ALLOWED_GITHUB_USERS` | No | Comma-separated GitHub usernames |\n| `ALLOWED_GITHUB_ORGS` | No | Comma-separated GitHub org logins |\n| `SESSION_SECRET` | Prod | Secret for HMAC-signing session cookie values |\n\n**Startup validation**: If `NO_AUTH` is not `true`, the server must refuse to start if both `ALLOWED_GITHUB_USERS` and `ALLOWED_GITHUB_ORGS` are empty. Log error and `os.Exit(1)`, consistent with existing startup failure patterns.\n\n**Note on allowlists**: If a user changes their GitHub username after being added to `ALLOWED_GITHUB_USERS`, they lose access. This is expected — update the allowlist accordingly.\n\n## Cookie Format\n\nThe session cookie value is `sessionID.hmacSignature`:\n- `sessionID` is the UUID from the `sessions` table\n- `hmacSignature` is `HMAC-SHA256(sessionID, SESSION_SECRET)`, hex-encoded\n- On validation: split cookie value at `.`, verify HMAC, then look up session in DB\n- This prevents session ID forgery even if an attacker can guess UUIDs\n\nCookie settings:\n| Setting | Value |\n|---------|-------|\n| Name | `session` |\n| HttpOnly | `true` |\n| Secure | `true` by default; set `SECURE_COOKIES=false` for local dev |\n| SameSite | `Lax` |\n| Path | `/` |\n\n**CSRF note**: `SameSite=Lax` prevents cookies from being sent on cross-origin POST requests, providing CSRF protection for the logout endpoint without needing a separate CSRF token.\n\n## Database Schema\n\nTwo new tables added to `internal/db/migrations.go`:\n\n```sql\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (\n    id              UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n    github_id       BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE,\n    github_login    VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,\n    name            VARCHAR(200),\n    avatar_url      TEXT,\n    created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),\n    updated_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()\n);\n\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sessions (\n    id          UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n    user_id     UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,\n    expires_at  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,\n    created_at  TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()\n);\nCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_sessions_user_id ON sessions(user_id);\nCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_sessions_expires_at ON sessions(expires_at);\n```\n\n- Users are upserted on each login (updates login, name, avatar_url, updated_at)\n- Sessions expire after 7 days, no sliding window — user re-authenticates after expiry\n- When a session expires mid-use, the next API call returns 401 and the frontend redirects to `/login`\n- Deleting a user cascades to their sessions\n\n### Future extensibility\n\n- Email/password: add `email`, `password_hash` columns to `users`\n- Google OAuth: add `google_id` column, new `/auth/google` routes\n- Multi-provider linking: add `user_identities(user_id, provider, provider_id, email)` table\n- Org-based access control: add `organizations` and `org_members` tables\n- None of these require changing the session or cookie mechanism\n\n## Backend Package: `internal/auth/`\n\n| File | Responsibility |\n|------|----------------|\n| `oauth.go` | GitHub OAuth: authorize redirect, callback, token exchange, user info fetch |\n| `session.go` | Session CRUD: create, validate (verify HMAC + DB lookup), delete, cleanup expired |\n| `middleware.go` | `RequireSession` middleware: check cookie, load user, inject into context |\n| `allowlist.go` | Parse env vars, check username/org membership, startup validation |\n\n### Auth middleware integration\n\nThe existing `Auth` middleware in `internal/api/auth.go` is modified to check two auth methods in order:\n\n1. **Bearer token** (existing): If `Authorization: Bearer ...` header is present, validate via `KeyStore.ValidateKey()`. If invalid, return 401.\n2. **Session cookie** (new): If no Bearer header, check for `session` cookie. Parse and verify HMAC signature, look up session in DB via a new `SessionStore` interface, load user. If invalid/expired, return 401.\n3. If neither is present, return 401.\n\n**`SessionStore` interface** (implemented by `internal/auth/session.go`):\n```go\ntype SessionStore interface {\n    ValidateSession(ctx context.Context, sessionID string) (*User, error)\n}\n```\n\n**Context key**: Authenticated user is stored in request context using `type contextKey string` with key `\"user\"`. API handlers access it via `auth.UserFromContext(ctx)` which returns `*auth.User` or nil.\n\n**`Auth` function signature** changes from `Auth(store KeyStore)` to `Auth(keys KeyStore, sessions SessionStore)`.\n\nRoute auth mapping:\n\n```\n/auth/github          → no auth (public)\n/auth/github/callback → no auth (public)\n/auth/me              → session cookie only (uses RequireSession)\n/auth/logout          → session cookie only (uses RequireSession)\n/api/v1/health        → no auth (existing)\n/api/v1/*             → API key OR session cookie (uses modified Auth)\n```\n\n## Frontend Changes\n\n### New files\n\n| File | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `web/app/login/page.tsx` | Login page: app logo, \"Sign in with GitHub\" button, error display |\n| `web/lib/auth/context.tsx` | `AuthProvider` + `useAuth()` hook |\n\n### Auth context\n\n```\nAuthProvider\n  → on mount: fetch /auth/me\n  → if 200: set user state, render children\n  → if 401 AND pathname is not /login: redirect to /login\n  → if on /login: render login page without redirect\n  → shows loading state while checking\n```\n\nThe `/login` page is excluded from the auth redirect loop — `AuthProvider` checks `window.location.pathname` before redirecting.\n\n### Layout changes (`web/app/layout.tsx`)\n\n- Wrap app in `AuthProvider`\n- Unauthenticated users are redirected to `/login` (except when already on `/login`)\n\n### Sidebar changes\n\n- Add user avatar + dropdown with \"Sign out\" option\n- Sign out POSTs to `/auth/logout`, redirects to `/login`\n\n### No changes to API client\n\n`web/lib/api/client.ts` uses `fetch()` which automatically sends `HttpOnly` cookies with same-origin requests. No Authorization header needed for session-based auth.\n\n## Development & NO_AUTH Mode\n\nWhen `NO_AUTH=true`:\n- API key auth bypass continues (existing)\n- Session auth middleware bypasses — injects a fake dev user\n- `/auth/me` returns a hardcoded dev user\n- No `GITHUB_CLIENT_ID` / `GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET` needed\n- Login page accessible but not required\n- Startup validation for allowlists is skipped\n\n## Session Cleanup\n\nA background goroutine runs hourly to delete expired sessions (`expires_at < NOW()`). Starts alongside the existing polling engine in `cmd/server/main.go`. Respects context cancellation for clean shutdown, using `time.Ticker`.\n\n## State Parameter Storage\n\nOAuth `state` values stored in an in-memory `sync.Map` with:\n- 10-minute TTL per entry\n- Maximum 1000 pending states (rejects new `/auth/github` requests with 429 if full)\n- Cleanup goroutine sweeps expired entries every 5 minutes\n- Sufficient for single-instance deployment; can be moved to PostgreSQL if multi-instance is needed later\n"
}

Plus incorporated suggestions: idempotent indexes, state map bounds, CSRF rationale, secure-by-default cookies, audit logging, and session expiry UX.

## Architecture Overview from These Files

**Backend (Go)**:
- API layer with middleware chain (auth, rate-limit, logging, CORS, panic recovery)
- Dependency injection via `Dependencies` struct passed to `RegisterRoutes`
- Database: PostgreSQL with River job queue
- Two worker types: `NotifyWorker` and `AgentWorker`

**Frontend (Next.js/React)**:
- Client-side SWR data fetching from API
- SSE for real-time updates
- Static export with dynamic route templates
- Custom fonts (Fraunces, DM Sans)

**Auth**: Bearer token validation via `KeyStore` interface; optional (NO_AUTH flag)

**Database Stores** (interface-based, likely implemented by `PgStore`):
- Projects, Sources, Releases, SemanticReleases, Subscriptions, Channels, ContextSources, Agents, Todos, Onboard, Keys, Health

     1→package db

    11→
    12→const schema = `
    13→-- Tracked software projects (the central entity)
    14→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS projects (
    15→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    16→    name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    17→    description TEXT,
    18→    agent_prompt TEXT,
    19→    agent_rules JSONB,
    20→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    21→    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
    22→);
    23→
    24→-- Configured ingestion sources (polling-based: GitHub, Docker Hub)
    25→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sources (
    26→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    27→    project_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    28→    provider VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    29→    repository VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
    30→    poll_interval_seconds INT DEFAULT 86400,
    31→    enabled BOOLEAN DEFAULT true,
    32→    config JSONB,
    33→    last_polled_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    34→    last_error TEXT,
    35→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    36→    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    37→    UNIQUE(provider, repository)
    38→);
    39→
    40→-- Context sources (read-only references for agent research)
    41→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS context_sources (
    42→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    43→    project_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    44→    type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    45→    name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    46→    config JSONB NOT NULL,
    47→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    48→    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
    49→);
    50→
    51→-- Source-level releases (detected from polling sources)
    52→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS releases (
    53→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    54→    source_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES sources(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    55→    version VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    56→    raw_data JSONB,
    57→    released_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    58→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    59→    UNIQUE(source_id, version)
    60→);
    61→
    62→-- Project-level semantic releases (AI-generated, correlating source releases)
    63→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS semantic_releases (
    64→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    65→    project_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    66→    version VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    67→    report JSONB,
    68→    status VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',
    69→    error TEXT,
    70→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    71→    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    72→    UNIQUE(project_id, version)
    73→);
    74→
    75→-- Join table: which source releases compose a semantic release
    76→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS semantic_release_sources (
    77→    semantic_release_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES semantic_releases(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    78→    release_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES releases(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    79→    PRIMARY KEY (semantic_release_id, release_id)
    80→);
    81→
    82→-- Notification channels (standalone)
    83→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS notification_channels (
    84→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    85→    name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    86→    type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    87→    config JSONB NOT NULL,
    88→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    89→    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
    90→);
    91→
    92→-- Subscriptions: two types (source releases and semantic releases)
    93→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS subscriptions (
    94→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    95→    channel_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES notification_channels(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    96→    type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL CHECK (type IN ('source_release', 'semantic_release')),
    97→    source_id UUID REFERENCES sources(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    98→    project_id UUID REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    99→    version_filter TEXT,
   100→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
   101→    CHECK (
   102→        (type = 'source_release'   AND source_id  IS NOT NULL AND project_id IS NULL) OR
   103→        (type = 'semantic_release' AND project_id IS NOT NULL AND source_id  IS NULL)
   104→    )
   105→);
   106→
   107→-- Agent runs (scoped to project)
   108→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS agent_runs (
   109→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
   110→    project_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
   111→    semantic_release_id UUID REFERENCES semantic_releases(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
   112→    trigger VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
   113→    status VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',
   114→    prompt_used TEXT,
   115→    error TEXT,
   116→    started_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
   117→    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
   118→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
   119→);
   120→
   121→ALTER TABLE agent_runs ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS version VARCHAR(100);
   122→
   123→-- API authentication keys
   124→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS api_keys (
   125→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
   126→    name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
   127→    key_hash VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
   128→    key_prefix VARCHAR(12) NOT NULL,
   129→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
   130→    last_used_at TIMESTAMPTZ
   131→);
   132→
   133→-- Trigger for SSE: notify on new releases
   134→CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION notify_release_created() RETURNS trigger AS $$
   135→BEGIN
   136→    PERFORM pg_notify('release_events', json_build_object('type', 'release', 'id', NEW.id)::text);
   137→    RETURN NEW;
   138→END;
   139→$$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;
   140→
   141→DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS release_created_trigger ON releases;
   142→CREATE TRIGGER release_created_trigger
   143→    AFTER INSERT ON releases
   144→    FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION notify_release_created();
   145→
   146→-- Trigger for SSE: notify on semantic release completion
   147→CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION notify_semantic_release() RETURNS trigger AS $$
   148→BEGIN
   149→    IF NEW.status = 'completed' AND (OLD IS NULL OR OLD.status != 'completed') THEN
   150→        PERFORM pg_notify('release_events', json_build_object('type', 'semantic_release', 'id', NEW.id)::text);
   151→    END IF;
   152→    RETURN NEW;
   153→END;
   154→$$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;
   155→
   156→DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS semantic_release_trigger ON semantic_releases;
   157→CREATE TRIGGER semantic_release_trigger
   158→    AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE ON semantic_releases
   159→    FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION notify_semantic_release();
   160→
   161→-- Release TODOs (acknowledge/resolve tracking)
   162→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS release_todos (
   163→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
   164→    release_id UUID REFERENCES releases(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
   165→    semantic_release_id UUID REFERENCES semantic_releases(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
   166→    status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',
   167→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
   168→    acknowledged_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
   169→    resolved_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
   170→    CHECK (
   171→        (release_id IS NOT NULL AND semantic_release_id IS NULL) OR
   172→        (release_id IS NULL AND semantic_release_id IS NOT NULL)
   173→    )
   174→);
   175→CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_release_todos_release_id ON release_todos(release_id) WHERE release_id IS NOT NULL;
   176→CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_release_todos_semantic_release_id ON release_todos(semantic_release_id) WHERE semantic_release_id IS NOT NULL;
   177→
   178→-- Onboarding scans (GitHub repo dependency detection)
   179→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS onboard_scans (
   180→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
   181→    repo_url TEXT NOT NULL,
   182→    status VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',
   183→    results JSONB,
   184→    error TEXT,
   185→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
   186→    started_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
   187→    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ
   188→);
   189→`
   190→
   191→// RunMigrations applies River's schema and the application schema. Idempotent — safe to call on every startup.
   192→func RunMigrations(ctx context.Context, pool *pgxpool.Pool) error {
   193→	migrator, err := rivermigrate.New(riverpgxv5.New(pool), nil)
   194→	if err != nil {
   195→		return fmt.Errorf("create river migrator: %w", err)
   196→	}
   197→	if _, err := migrator.Migrate(ctx, rivermigrate.DirectionUp, nil); err != nil {
   198→		return fmt.Errorf("river migrations: %w", err)
   199→	}
   200→
   201→	if _, err := pool.Exec(ctx, schema); err != nil {
   202→		return fmt.Errorf("app migrations: %w", err)
   203→	}
   204→
   205→	// Migrate existing subscription type values: source→source_release, project→semantic_release.
   206→	// Drop old CHECK constraints first (both the named type check and the unnamed
   207→	// composite check referencing old values), then UPDATE data, then re-add constraints.
   208→	if _, err := pool.Exec(ctx, `
   209→		ALTER TABLE subscriptions DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS subscriptions_type_check;
   210→		ALTER TABLE subscriptions DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS subscriptions_check;
   211→		UPDATE subscriptions SET type = 'source_release' WHERE type = 'source';
   212→		UPDATE subscriptions SET type = 'semantic_release' WHERE type = 'project';
   213→		ALTER TABLE subscriptions ADD CONSTRAINT subscriptions_type_check CHECK (type IN ('source_release', 'semantic_release'));
   214→		ALTER TABLE subscriptions ADD CONSTRAINT subscriptions_check CHECK (
   215→			(type = 'source_release'   AND source_id IS NOT NULL AND project_id IS NULL) OR
   216→			(type = 'semantic_release' AND project_id IS NOT NULL AND source_id IS NULL)
   217→		);
   218→	`); err != nil {
   219→		return fmt.Errorf("subscription type migration: %w", err)
   220→	}
   221→
   222→	if _, err := pool.Exec(ctx, `
   223→		ALTER TABLE sources ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS version_filter_include TEXT;
   224→		ALTER TABLE sources ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS version_filter_exclude TEXT;
   225→	`); err != nil {
   226→		return fmt.Errorf("source version filter migration: %w", err)
   227→	}
   228→
   229→	if _, err := pool.Exec(ctx, `
   230→		ALTER TABLE sources ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS exclude_prereleases BOOLEAN DEFAULT false;
   231→	`); err != nil {
   232→		return fmt.Errorf("source exclude_prereleases migration: %w", err)
   233→	}
   234→	return nil
   235→}

     1→package main
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"log/slog"
     6→	"net/http"
     7→	"os"
     8→	"os/signal"
     9→	"time"
    10→
    11→	"github.com/riverqueue/river"
    12→	agentpkg "github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/agent"
    13→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/api"
    14→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/db"
    15→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/ingestion"
    16→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/onboard"
    17→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/queue"
    18→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/routing"
    19→)
    20→
    21→func main() {
    22→	// Configure log level from LOG_LEVEL env (debug, info, warn, error).
    23→	logLevel := new(slog.LevelVar) // defaults to Info
    24→	switch os.Getenv("LOG_LEVEL") {
    25→	case "debug":
    26→		logLevel.Set(slog.LevelDebug)
    27→	case "warn":
    28→		logLevel.Set(slog.LevelWarn)
    29→	case "error":
    30→		logLevel.Set(slog.LevelError)
    31→	}
    32→	slog.SetDefault(slog.New(slog.NewTextHandler(os.Stderr, &slog.HandlerOptions{Level: logLevel})))
    33→
    34→	ctx, stop := signal.NotifyContext(context.Background(), os.Interrupt)
    35→	defer stop()
    36→
    37→	dbURL := envOr("DATABASE_URL", "postgres://localhost:5432/changelogue?sslmode=disable")
    38→	addr := envOr("LISTEN_ADDR", ":8080")
    39→	noAuth := os.Getenv("NO_AUTH") == "true"
    40→
    41→	// Database
    42→	pool, err := db.NewPool(ctx, dbURL)
    43→	if err != nil {
    44→		slog.Error("database connection failed", "err", err)
    45→		os.Exit(1)
    46→	}
    47→	defer pool.Close()
    48→
    49→	if err := db.RunMigrations(ctx, pool); err != nil {
    50→		slog.Error("migrations failed", "err", err)
    51→		os.Exit(1)
    52→	}
    53→
    54→	// API & routing store (created before River so we can register workers)
    55→	// The river client is set to nil initially; TriggerAgentRun (which needs it)
    56→	// won't be called until the server is up and the client is set below.
    57→	pgStore := api.NewPgStore(pool, nil)
    58→
    59→	// Register River workers
    60→	workers := river.NewWorkers()
    61→	notifyWorker := routing.NewNotifyWorker(pgStore, os.Getenv("PUBLIC_URL"))
    62→	river.AddWorker(workers, notifyWorker)
    63→
    64→	// Agent worker: requires an LLM API key. If unavailable, agent jobs
    65→	// will remain in the queue until the key is configured and the server
    66→	// is restarted.
    67→	llmProvider := envOr("LLM_PROVIDER", "gemini")
    68→	llmModelDefault := "gemini-2.5-flash"
    69→	if llmProvider == "openai" {
    70→		llmModelDefault = "gpt-5.2"
    71→	}
    72→	llmConfig := agentpkg.LLMConfig{
    73→		Provider:      llmProvider,
    74→		Model:         envOr("LLM_MODEL", llmModelDefault),
    75→		GoogleAPIKey:  os.Getenv("GOOGLE_API_KEY"),
    76→		OpenAIAPIKey:  os.Getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY"),
    77→		OpenAIBaseURL: os.Getenv("OPENAI_BASE_URL"),
    78→	}
    79→	agentOrchestrator, err := agentpkg.NewOrchestrator(pgStore, llmConfig, os.Getenv("PUBLIC_URL"))
    80→	if err != nil {
    81→		slog.Warn("agent orchestrator not available — agent jobs will not be processed", "err", err)
    82→	} else {
    83→		agentWorker := agentpkg.NewAgentWorker(agentOrchestrator, pgStore)
    84→		river.AddWorker(workers, agentWorker)
    85→		slog.Info("agent worker registered")
    86→	}
    87→
    88→	// Scan worker: requires LLM for dependency extraction
    89→	scanScanner := onboard.NewScanner(http.DefaultClient, "", "")
    90→	var scanExtractor *onboard.DependencyExtractor
    91→	extractorCfg := onboard.ExtractorConfig{
    92→		Provider:      llmConfig.Provider,
    93→		Model:         llmConfig.Model,
    94→		GoogleAPIKey:  llmConfig.GoogleAPIKey,
    95→		OpenAIAPIKey:  llmConfig.OpenAIAPIKey,
    96→		OpenAIBaseURL: llmConfig.OpenAIBaseURL,
    97→	}
    98→	ext, err := onboard.NewDependencyExtractor(ctx, extractorCfg)
    99→	if err != nil {
   100→		slog.Warn("dependency extractor not available", "err", err)
   101→	} else {
   102→		scanExtractor = ext
   103→	}
   104→	scanWorker := onboard.NewScanWorker(pgStore, scanScanner, scanExtractor, pool)
   105→	river.AddWorker(workers, scanWorker)
   106→	slog.Info("scan worker registered")
   107→
   108→	riverClient, err := queue.NewRiverClient(pool, workers)
   109→	if err != nil {
   110→		slog.Error("river client failed", "err", err)
   111→		os.Exit(1)
   112→	}
   113→
   114→	// Now that the river client exists, set it on the existing pgStore so that
   115→	// all holders (including NotifyWorker) can enqueue agent jobs.
   116→	pgStore.SetRiverClient(riverClient)
   117→
   118→	if err := riverClient.Start(ctx); err != nil {
   119→		slog.Error("river start failed", "err", err)
   120→		os.Exit(1)
   121→	}
   122→
   123→	// Ingestion layer
   124→	ingestionStore := ingestion.NewPgStore(pool, riverClient)
   125→	svc := ingestion.NewService(ingestionStore)
   126→
   127→	loader := ingestion.NewSourceLoader(pool, http.DefaultClient)
   128→	orch := ingestion.NewOrchestrator(svc, loader, pool, 5*time.Minute)
   129→
   130→	broadcaster := api.NewBroadcaster()
   131→
   132→	mux := http.NewServeMux()
   133→
   134→	// Serve frontend static export if available
   135→	if info, err := os.Stat("web/out"); err == nil && info.IsDir() {
   136→		api.RegisterFrontend(mux, "web/out")
   137→		slog.Info("serving frontend from web/out")
   138→	}
   139→
   140→	// Register all API v1 routes
   141→	api.RegisterRoutes(mux, api.Dependencies{
   142→		DB:                    pool,
   143→		ProjectsStore:         pgStore,
   144→		ReleasesStore:         pgStore,
   145→		SubscriptionsStore:    pgStore,
   146→		SourcesStore:          pgStore,
   147→		ChannelsStore:         pgStore,
   148→		ContextSourcesStore:   pgStore,
   149→		SemanticReleasesStore: pgStore,
   150→		AgentStore:            pgStore,
   151→		TodosStore:            pgStore,
   152→		OnboardStore:          pgStore,
   153→		PublicURL:             os.Getenv("PUBLIC_URL"),
   154→		KeyStore:              pgStore,
   155→		HealthChecker:         pgStore,
   156→		Broadcaster:           broadcaster,
   157→		NoAuth:                noAuth,
   158→		IngestionService:      svc,
   159→		HTTPClient:            http.DefaultClient,
   160→	})
   161→
   162→	srv := &http.Server{Addr: addr, Handler: api.CORS(mux)}
   163→
   164→	// Start polling in background
   165→	go orch.Run(ctx)
   166→
   167→	// Start PostgreSQL LISTEN/NOTIFY → SSE bridge
   168→	go api.ListenForNotifications(ctx, pool, broadcaster)
   169→
   170→	// Start HTTP server
   171→	go func() {
   172→		slog.Info("server starting", "addr", srv.Addr)
   173→		if err := srv.ListenAndServe(); err != http.ErrServerClosed {
   174→			slog.Error("server error", "err", err)
   175→		}
   176→	}()
   177→
   178→	<-ctx.Done()
   179→	slog.Info("shutting down")
   180→
   181→	shutdownCtx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
   182→	defer cancel()
   183→	riverClient.Stop(shutdownCtx)
   184→	srv.Shutdown(shutdownCtx)
   185→}
   186→
   187→func envOr(key, fallback string) string {
   188→	if v := os.Getenv(key); v != "" {
   189→		return v
   190→	}
   191→	return fallback
   192→}
   193→

     1→package api
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"net/http"
     6→	"strings"
     7→	"sync"
     8→
     9→	"golang.org/x/time/rate"
    10→)
    11→
    12→type KeyStore interface {
    13→	ValidateKey(ctx context.Context, rawKey string) (bool, error)
    14→	TouchKeyUsage(ctx context.Context, rawKey string)
    15→}
    16→
    17→func Auth(store KeyStore) Middleware {
    18→	return func(next http.Handler) http.Handler {
    19→		return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    20→			header := r.Header.Get("Authorization")
    21→			if !strings.HasPrefix(header, "Bearer ") {
    22→				RespondError(w, r, http.StatusUnauthorized, "unauthorized", "Missing API key")
    23→				return
    24→			}
    25→			rawKey := strings.TrimPrefix(header, "Bearer ")
    26→			valid, err := store.ValidateKey(r.Context(), rawKey)
    27→			if err != nil || !valid {
    28→				RespondError(w, r, http.StatusUnauthorized, "unauthorized", "Invalid API key")
    29→				return
    30→			}
    31→			go store.TouchKeyUsage(context.Background(), rawKey)
    32→			next.ServeHTTP(w, r)
    33→		})
    34→	}
    35→}
    36→
    37→func RateLimit(rps float64, burst int) Middleware {
    38→	var mu sync.Mutex
    39→	limiters := make(map[string]*rate.Limiter)
    40→	getLimiter := func(key string) *rate.Limiter {
    41→		mu.Lock()
    42→		defer mu.Unlock()
    43→		if lim, ok := limiters[key]; ok {
    44→			return lim
    45→		}
    46→		lim := rate.NewLimiter(rate.Limit(rps), burst)
    47→		limiters[key] = lim
    48→		return lim
    49→	}
    50→	return func(next http.Handler) http.Handler {
    51→		return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    52→			key := r.RemoteAddr
    53→			if auth := r.Header.Get("Authorization"); strings.HasPrefix(auth, "Bearer ") {
    54→				key = strings.TrimPrefix(auth, "Bearer ")
    55→			}
    56→			lim := getLimiter(key)
    57→			if !lim.Allow() {
    58→				w.Header().Set("Retry-After", "1")
    59→				RespondError(w, r, http.StatusTooManyRequests, "rate_limited", "Too many requests")
    60→				return
    61→			}
    62→			next.ServeHTTP(w, r)
    63→		})
    64→	}
    65→}
    66→

     1→package api
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"net/http"
     5→
     6→	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"
     7→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/ingestion"
     8→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/routing"
     9→)
    10→
    11→// Dependencies holds all external dependencies required for registering API routes.
    12→type Dependencies struct {
    13→	DB                    *pgxpool.Pool
    14→	ProjectsStore         ProjectsStore
    15→	ReleasesStore         ReleasesStore
    16→	SubscriptionsStore    SubscriptionsStore
    17→	SourcesStore          SourcesStore
    18→	ChannelsStore         ChannelsStore
    19→	ContextSourcesStore   ContextSourcesStore
    20→	SemanticReleasesStore SemanticReleasesStore
    21→	AgentStore            AgentStore
    22→	TodosStore            TodosStore
    23→	OnboardStore          OnboardStore
    24→	PublicURL             string
    25→	KeyStore              KeyStore
    26→	HealthChecker         HealthChecker
    27→	Broadcaster           *Broadcaster
    28→	NoAuth                bool
    29→	IngestionService      *ingestion.Service
    30→	HTTPClient            *http.Client
    31→}
    32→
    33→// RegisterRoutes registers all API v1 routes on the given ServeMux.
    34→func RegisterRoutes(mux *http.ServeMux, deps Dependencies) {
    35→	// Public chain: no auth required.
    36→	publicChain := Chain(RequestID, Logger, Recovery)
    37→
    38→	// Authenticated chain: includes auth unless NoAuth is set.
    39→	chain := publicChain
    40→	if !deps.NoAuth {
    41→		chain = Chain(RequestID, Logger, Recovery, RateLimit(10, 20), Auth(deps.KeyStore))
    42→	}
    43→
    44→	// Projects (CRUD)
    45→	projects := NewProjectsHandler(deps.ProjectsStore)
    46→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/projects", chain(http.HandlerFunc(projects.List)))
    47→	mux.Handle("POST /api/v1/projects", chain(http.HandlerFunc(projects.Create)))
    48→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/projects/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(projects.Get)))
    49→	mux.Handle("PUT /api/v1/projects/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(projects.Update)))
    50→	mux.Handle("DELETE /api/v1/projects/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(projects.Delete)))
    51→
    52→	// Sources (nested under projects)
    53→	sources := NewSourcesHandler(deps.SourcesStore, deps.IngestionService, deps.HTTPClient)
    54→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/sources", chain(http.HandlerFunc(sources.List)))
    55→	mux.Handle("POST /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/sources", chain(http.HandlerFunc(sources.Create)))
    56→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/sources/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(sources.Get)))
    57→	mux.Handle("PUT /api/v1/sources/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(sources.Update)))
    58→	mux.Handle("DELETE /api/v1/sources/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(sources.Delete)))
    59→	mux.Handle("POST /api/v1/sources/{id}/poll", chain(http.HandlerFunc(sources.FetchReleases)))
    60→
    61→	// Context Sources (nested under projects)
    62→	contextSources := NewContextSourcesHandler(deps.ContextSourcesStore)
    63→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/context-sources", chain(http.HandlerFunc(contextSources.List)))
    64→	mux.Handle("POST /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/context-sources", chain(http.HandlerFunc(contextSources.Create)))
    65→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/context-sources/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(contextSources.Get)))
    66→	mux.Handle("PUT /api/v1/context-sources/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(contextSources.Update)))
    67→	mux.Handle("DELETE /api/v1/context-sources/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(contextSources.Delete)))
    68→
    69→	// Releases (read-only, nested under sources and projects)
    70→	releases := NewReleasesHandler(deps.ReleasesStore)
    71→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/releases", chain(http.HandlerFunc(releases.List)))
    72→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/sources/{id}/releases", chain(http.HandlerFunc(releases.ListBySource)))
    73→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/releases", chain(http.HandlerFunc(releases.ListByProject)))
    74→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/releases/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(releases.Get)))
    75→
    76→	// Semantic Releases (nested under projects + top-level list)
    77→	semanticReleases := NewSemanticReleasesHandler(deps.SemanticReleasesStore)
    78→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/semantic-releases", chain(http.HandlerFunc(semanticReleases.ListAll)))
    79→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/semantic-releases", chain(http.HandlerFunc(semanticReleases.List)))
    80→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/semantic-releases/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(semanticReleases.Get)))
    81→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/semantic-releases/{id}/sources", chain(http.HandlerFunc(semanticReleases.ListSources)))
    82→	mux.Handle("DELETE /api/v1/semantic-releases/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(semanticReleases.Delete)))
    83→
    84→	// Subscriptions (CRUD)
    85→	subscriptions := NewSubscriptionsHandler(deps.SubscriptionsStore)
    86→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/subscriptions", chain(http.HandlerFunc(subscriptions.List)))
    87→	mux.Handle("POST /api/v1/subscriptions", chain(http.HandlerFunc(subscriptions.Create)))
    88→	mux.Handle("POST /api/v1/subscriptions/batch", chain(http.HandlerFunc(subscriptions.BatchCreate)))
    89→	mux.Handle("DELETE /api/v1/subscriptions/batch", chain(http.HandlerFunc(subscriptions.BatchDelete)))
    90→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/subscriptions/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(subscriptions.Get)))
    91→	mux.Handle("PUT /api/v1/subscriptions/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(subscriptions.Update)))
    92→	mux.Handle("DELETE /api/v1/subscriptions/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(subscriptions.Delete)))
    93→
    94→	// Channels (CRUD + test)
    95→	channels := NewChannelsHandler(deps.ChannelsStore, routing.NewSenders(), deps.PublicURL)
    96→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/channels", chain(http.HandlerFunc(channels.List)))
    97→	mux.Handle("POST /api/v1/channels", chain(http.HandlerFunc(channels.Create)))
    98→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/channels/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(channels.Get)))
    99→	mux.Handle("PUT /api/v1/channels/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(channels.Update)))
   100→	mux.Handle("DELETE /api/v1/channels/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(channels.Delete)))
   101→	mux.Handle("POST /api/v1/channels/{id}/test", chain(http.HandlerFunc(channels.Test)))
   102→
   103→	// Agent
   104→	agent := NewAgentHandler(deps.AgentStore)
   105→	mux.Handle("POST /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/agent/run", chain(http.HandlerFunc(agent.TriggerRun)))
   106→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/agent/runs", chain(http.HandlerFunc(agent.ListRuns)))
   107→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/agent-runs/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(agent.GetRun)))
   108→
   109→	// Todos
   110→	todos := NewTodosHandler(deps.TodosStore, deps.PublicURL)
   111→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/todos", chain(http.HandlerFunc(todos.List)))
   112→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/todos/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(todos.Get)))
   113→	mux.Handle("PATCH /api/v1/todos/{id}/acknowledge", chain(http.HandlerFunc(todos.Acknowledge)))
   114→	mux.Handle("PATCH /api/v1/todos/{id}/resolve", chain(http.HandlerFunc(todos.Resolve)))
   115→	mux.Handle("PATCH /api/v1/todos/{id}/reopen", chain(http.HandlerFunc(todos.Reopen)))
   116→	// One-click endpoints for notification links (GET so they work as <a href>)
   117→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/todos/{id}/acknowledge", chain(http.HandlerFunc(todos.Acknowledge)))
   118→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/todos/{id}/resolve", chain(http.HandlerFunc(todos.Resolve)))
   119→
   120→	// Onboard (repo dependency scanning)
   121→	ob := NewOnboardHandler(deps.OnboardStore)
   122→	mux.Handle("POST /api/v1/onboard/scan", chain(http.HandlerFunc(ob.Scan)))
   123→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}", chain(http.HandlerFunc(ob.GetScan)))
   124→	mux.Handle("POST /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}/apply", chain(http.HandlerFunc(ob.Apply)))
   125→
   126→	// Providers (metadata — static, no store needed)
   127→	providers := NewProvidersHandler()
   128→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/providers", chain(http.HandlerFunc(providers.List)))
   129→
   130→	// Discovery (public — no auth, proxies external APIs)
   131→	discover := NewDiscoverHandler(deps.HTTPClient, "", "")
   132→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/discover/github", publicChain(http.HandlerFunc(discover.GitHub)))
   133→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/discover/dockerhub", publicChain(http.HandlerFunc(discover.DockerHub)))
   134→
   135→	// SSE events
   136→	events := NewEventsHandler(deps.Broadcaster)
   137→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/events", chain(http.HandlerFunc(events.Stream)))
   138→
   139→	// Health (public — no auth middleware)
   140→	health := NewHealthHandler(deps.HealthChecker)
   141→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/health", publicChain(http.HandlerFunc(health.Check)))
   142→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/stats", chain(http.HandlerFunc(health.Stats)))
   143→	mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/stats/trend", chain(http.HandlerFunc(health.Trend)))
   144→}
