> AGENT

Fixed. The NO_AUTH `/auth/github` handler now redirects to `FRONTEND_URL` (e.g. `http://localhost:3001/`) instead of just `/` (which landed on `localhost:8080`).

Restart with `make run` (for NO_AUTH) or `make run-auth` (for real OAuth) and try the login flow again.

> DEVELOPER

It redirects to http://localhost:8080/login

> AGENT

The login page's "Sign in with GitHub" link uses `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` to point to the backend (`http://localhost:8080`). After the backend clears the cookie, it redirects to `FRONTEND_URL`. But the browser is now on `localhost:8080`, setting the cookie on that domain — then the redirect to `localhost:3001` is a different origin, so the cookie doesn't carry over.

The real problem: in NO_AUTH mode with separate ports, using a full-page navigation to the backend and back creates cross-origin cookie issues. The simpler approach is to handle the NO_AUTH "login" entirely on the frontend with a `fetch()` call, same as logout.

> AGENT

The right approach: the login button should use `/auth/github` through the Next.js rewrite proxy (not a direct link to the backend). In production it's same-origin so it works. In dev, the rewrite proxies it. The problem we hit earlier was that `/auth/github` is a navigation that returns a 302 — Next.js rewrites handle that by proxying the response back, so the browser sees the 302 from `localhost:3001` and follows the redirect.

The fix is simple — remove `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` from the login link and just use `/auth/github`. The rewrite proxy handles the rest.

Looking at the current state, the last issue was that clicking "Sign in with GitHub" redirected to `http://localhost:8080/login`. I changed the href back to `/auth/github` to go through the Next.js rewrite proxy, but there's a fundamental problem: `<a href>` browser navigations that get a 302 from the proxied backend may not behave correctly through Next.js rewrites.

The cleanest fix for the NO_AUTH dev mode is to make the login button use JavaScript navigation instead of an `<a href>`, similar to how logout works. This way the request goes through Next.js's rewrite proxy as a fetch, and we handle the redirect client-side.

```
    11→import { usePathname, useRouter } from "next/navigation";

    13→interface User {
    14→  id: string;
    15→  github_id: number;
    16→  github_login: string;
    17→  name?: string;
    18→  avatar_url?: string;
    19→}
    20→
    21→interface AuthContextValue {
    22→  user: User | null;
    23→  loading: boolean;
    24→  logout: () => Promise<void>;
    25→}
    26→
    27→const AuthContext = createContext<AuthContextValue>({
    28→  user: null,
    29→  loading: true,
    30→  logout: async () => {},
    31→});
    32→
    33→export function useAuth() {
    34→  return useContext(AuthContext);
    35→}
    36→
    37→export function AuthProvider({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
    38→  const [user, setUser] = useState<User | null>(null);
    39→  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);
    40→  const pathname = usePathname();
    41→  const router = useRouter();
    42→  const didFetch = useRef(false);
    43→
    44→  useEffect(() => {
    45→    if (didFetch.current) return;
    46→    didFetch.current = true;
    47→
    48→    fetch("/auth/me")
    49→      .then((res) => {
    50→        if (res.ok) return res.json();
    51→        throw new Error("unauthorized");
    52→      })
    53→      .then((data) => {
    54→        setUser(data);
    55→        setLoading(false);
    56→      })
    57→      .catch(() => {
    58→        setUser(null);
    59→        setLoading(false);
    60→      });
    61→  }, []);
    62→
    63→  // Redirect to /login when not authenticated and not already on /login
    64→  useEffect(() => {
    65→    if (!loading && !user && pathname !== "/login") {
    66→      router.replace("/login");
    67→    }
    68→  }, [loading, user, pathname, router]);
    69→
    70→  const logout = async () => {
    71→    await fetch("/auth/logout", { method: "POST" });
    72→    setUser(null);
    73→    router.replace("/login");
    74→  };
    75→
    76→  if (loading) {
    77→    return (
    78→      <div className="flex h-screen items-center justify-center bg-[#f8f8f6]">
    79→        <div className="h-6 w-6 animate-spin rounded-full border-2 border-[#e8601a] border-t-transparent" />
    80→      </div>
    81→    );
    82→  }
    83→
    84→  // On login page, render without redirect guard
    85→  if (pathname === "/login") {
    86→    return (
    87→      <AuthContext.Provider value={{ user, loading, logout }}>
    88→        {children}
    89→      </AuthContext.Provider>
    90→    );
    91→  }
    92→
    93→  // Not logged in and not on login page — handled by useEffect redirect above
    94→  if (!user) {
    95→    return null;
    96→  }
    97→
    98→  return (
    99→    <AuthContext.Provider value={{ user, loading, logout }}>
   100→      {children}
   101→    </AuthContext.Provider>
   102→  );
   103→}
```

> AGENT

The core problem: `<a href="/auth/github">` is a full browser navigation. When Next.js receives this, it tries to handle it as a page route, not as a rewrite proxy target. Next.js rewrites only apply to `fetch()` API calls, not to full-page navigations handled by the Next.js router.

The fix: convert the login button to use `window.location.href` via JavaScript, which triggers a full browser navigation that bypasses the Next.js router and hits the Next.js dev server directly (where the rewrite applies at the HTTP level).

Actually, let me reconsider. Next.js rewrites DO apply at the HTTP server level for all requests, not just `fetch()`. The issue is that `/auth/github` might be caught by the Next.js file-system router first. Let me check if there's an `/auth` page or catch-all route.

Good — there's no `web/app/auth/` directory, so Next.js won't intercept `/auth/github` as a page route. The rewrite should work for full-page navigations too since there's no matching page.

The real problem in the previous session was: when `<a href="/auth/github">` is clicked, the browser navigates to `localhost:3001/auth/github`. Next.js rewrites this to `localhost:8080/auth/github`. The Go backend (NO_AUTH mode) responds with a **302 redirect** to `FRONTEND_URL` (localhost:3001). The Next.js rewrite proxy transparently returns that 302 to the browser. The browser follows the 302 to `localhost:3001/` — which should work.

But the user reported being redirected to `http://localhost:8080/login`. That would happen if the backend's NO_AUTH `/auth/github` handler was redirecting to `localhost:8080/login` instead of `localhost:3001/`. Let me re-read the current handler code.

Looking at `internal/api/server.go:168-181`:
```go
mux.HandleFunc("GET /auth/github", func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    http.SetCookie(w, &http.Cookie{Name: "dev_logged_out", Value: "", Path: "/", MaxAge: -1})
    target := "/"
    if frontendURL != "" {
        target = strings.TrimRight(frontendURL, "/") + "/"
    }
    http.Redirect(w, r, target, http.StatusFound)
})
```

This should redirect to `http://localhost:3001/` if `FRONTEND_URL=http://localhost:3001`. But if running with `make run` (not `make run-auth`), `FRONTEND_URL` won't be set, and it redirects to `/` — which the browser interprets as `localhost:8080/` since the 302 comes from the proxied request.

Wait — in NO_AUTH mode with `make run`, `FRONTEND_URL` is not passed. Let me check.

> AGENT

I see the issue. In `make run`, `FRONTEND_URL` is not set. The Go server reads `os.Getenv("FRONTEND_URL")` which returns `""`. So the NO_AUTH `/auth/github` handler's `frontendURL` parameter is `""`, and it redirects to just `/`. 

When the browser navigates to `localhost:3001/auth/github`, Next.js rewrites to `localhost:8080/auth/github`, the backend responds with `302 Location: /`. The Next.js rewrite proxy sees a relative redirect `/` — this gets sent back to the browser as a redirect to `localhost:3001/`. That should actually work!

But the user reported going to `http://localhost:8080/login`. That could only happen if the login button had the `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` prefix pointing to `localhost:8080`. I already fixed that (the current code shows `href="/auth/github"`). 

The real issue now is: in NO_AUTH mode with `make run`, clicking "Sign in with GitHub" on `/login` page should:
1. Navigate to `localhost:3001/auth/github`
2. Next.js rewrites to `localhost:8080/auth/github` 
3. Backend clears `dev_logged_out` cookie and responds with `302 → /`
4. Browser follows redirect to `localhost:3001/`
5. AuthProvider fetches `/auth/me` → gets dev user → dashboard loads

But there's a cookie problem: the `dev_logged_out` cookie was set by the backend when proxied from the Next.js dev server. When the backend response comes back through the proxy, the cookie is set on `localhost:3001` domain. But in step 3, the `Set-Cookie` to clear `dev_logged_out` is also coming through the proxy, so it should also be on `localhost:3001`. That should work.

However, the `AuthProvider` has `didFetch.current = true` after the initial fetch. After the redirect from `/auth/github` back to `/`, the page does a full reload (302 redirect causes full page load), so `didFetch` resets. This should work.

Let me also check: is there a problem where the `dev_logged_out` cookie set during the fetch-based logout doesn't get cleared during the full-page navigation login? The logout does `fetch("/auth/logout", { method: "POST" })` which sets the `dev_logged_out=1` cookie on `localhost:3001`. Then login navigates to `/auth/github`, which goes through the rewrite and the backend's `Set-Cookie` clears `dev_logged_out` — but `Set-Cookie` on a 302 response through a proxy may not work correctly because the browser may not process cookies on redirect responses from a proxy.

This is the real problem. Let me fix this properly. The `<a href>` approach through the Next.js proxy is fragile. Instead, I'll make the login button use JavaScript:

1. Use `onClick` handler that does a `fetch("/auth/github", { redirect: "manual" })` to clear the `dev_logged_out` cookie
2. Then navigate to `/` via `window.location`

Actually, even simpler — for NO_AUTH mode, the login just needs to clear the `dev_logged_out` cookie and redirect to home. I can handle this entirely client-side in the AuthProvider/login page without touching the backend at all. But we need it to work for real auth too (where `/auth/github` initiates the OAuth flow).

The cleanest approach: make the login button use `window.location.href = "/auth/github"` which triggers a real browser navigation through the Next.js dev server (not the router). For real auth, the Next.js server proxies to the backend which returns a 302 to GitHub. For NO_AUTH, it proxies to the backend which clears the cookie and redirects back.

The `<a href>` already does this. Let me think about what's different...

Actually, `<a href="/auth/github">` IS a full browser navigation. It goes to the Next.js HTTP server at `localhost:3001/auth/github`. The Next.js dev server applies the rewrite and proxies to `localhost:8080/auth/github`. The backend returns a 302. The key question is: does Next.js's rewrite proxy follow the redirect internally, or pass it through to the browser?

According to Next.js docs, rewrites are transparent — they proxy the request and return the response as-is. So a 302 from the backend gets returned as a 302 to the browser. The `Location` header from the backend is `/` (relative), which the browser resolves relative to the current URL origin (`localhost:3001`), so it goes to `localhost:3001/`.

This should work. The previous problem was that the `href` had the `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` prefix, sending the browser directly to `localhost:8080`. That's already fixed. Let me also make sure `make run` passes `FRONTEND_URL` so the backend redirect is correct even if the proxy doesn't handle relative redirects well.

> AGENT

Backend builds clean. Let me verify the full login flow logic is consistent across both modes:

**NO_AUTH mode (`make run`):**
1. User lands on `/` → AuthProvider fetches `/auth/me` → dev user returned → dashboard shown
2. User clicks logout → `POST /auth/logout` sets `dev_logged_out=1` cookie → AuthProvider sets user null → redirected to `/login`
3. User clicks "Sign in with GitHub" → JS clears `dev_logged_out` cookie client-side → `window.location.href = "/auth/github"` → Next.js rewrites to backend → backend also clears cookie + redirects to `/` (or `FRONTEND_URL/`) → browser follows 302 → AuthProvider fetches `/auth/me` → no `dev_logged_out` cookie → dev user returned → dashboard

**Real auth mode (`make run-auth`):**
1. User lands on `/` → AuthProvider fetches `/auth/me` → 401 → redirected to `/login`
2. User clicks "Sign in with GitHub" → JS clears `dev_logged_out` cookie (no-op, doesn't exist) → `window.location.href = "/auth/github"` → Next.js rewrites to backend → backend redirects to GitHub OAuth → user authorizes → callback → session cookie set → redirected to `FRONTEND_URL/`

This is solid. The key improvement: clearing the `dev_logged_out` cookie client-side before navigating ensures the flow works even if the proxy doesn't pass through `Set-Cookie` from the 302 response.

> AGENT

Committed. Here's a summary of what was fixed:

**Login button**: Changed from `<a href>` to `<button onClick>` that:
1. Clears the `dev_logged_out` cookie client-side (for NO_AUTH mode)
2. Uses `window.location.href = "/auth/github"` for full-page navigation

This fixes the dev proxy issue because:
- The `dev_logged_out` cookie is cleared before navigation, so even if the proxy doesn't forward `Set-Cookie` from the backend's 302 response, the cookie is already gone
- `window.location.href` triggers a real browser navigation to the Next.js dev server, which applies the rewrite to the Go backend
- Removed `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` prefix so the navigation stays on `localhost:3001` (goes through proxy) instead of going directly to `localhost:8080`

**Makefile**: Added `FRONTEND_URL` forwarding to `make run`, and the `run-auth` target for testing real GitHub OAuth.

To test locally:
- `make run` + `make frontend-dev` — NO_AUTH mode, login/logout cycle should work
- `make run-auth` with GitHub OAuth env vars — real auth mode, redirects to GitHub

> AGENT

The issue is that `make run-auth` doesn't set `FRONTEND_URL`, so the backend redirects to `/` which resolves to `localhost:8080` (the backend port). And the login button's `window.location.href = "/auth/github"` goes through the Next.js proxy, but the backend's 302 response has `Location: /` — and depending on how the proxy handles it, the browser may end up at the backend URL.

Let me trace the actual problem more carefully. The `handleLogin` does `window.location.href = "/auth/github"`. This is a browser navigation to `localhost:3001/auth/github`. Next.js rewrites to `localhost:8080/auth/github`. But here's the thing — this is **real auth mode**, not NO_AUTH. So the backend's `HandleGitHubRedirect` should return a 302 to `https://github.com/login/oauth/authorize?...`. The browser should follow that to GitHub.

But you said it redirects to `http://localhost:8080/`. That means the backend is NOT running in auth mode — it's still in NO_AUTH mode somehow. Let me check.

> AGENT

I see the problem. The `run-auth` target doesn't set `NO_AUTH=true`, which is correct — auth is enabled. But `FRONTEND_URL` is `$(FRONTEND_URL)`, and you didn't pass `FRONTEND_URL` on the command line, so it's empty. That means:

1. The OAuth flow works — `/auth/github` redirects to GitHub, GitHub calls back, session cookie is set
2. After successful login, `HandleGitHubCallback` calls `h.frontendURL("/")` which returns just `/` (since `FrontendURL` is empty)
3. The backend sends `302 Location: /` — the browser resolves this to `http://localhost:8080/`

And there's another issue: the callback URL sent to GitHub is `http://localhost:8080/auth/github/callback` (based on the request's Host header). When the Next.js proxy forwards the request, the `Host` header might be `localhost:8080` (the backend), not `localhost:3001`. Let me check the `callbackURL` function.

> AGENT

Right. The `callbackURL` uses `r.Host` which, when proxied through Next.js, will be `localhost:8080` (the destination of the rewrite), not `localhost:3001`. This means:
- GitHub's callback will go to `http://localhost:8080/auth/github/callback` (directly to the backend, bypassing the frontend)
- After the callback succeeds, the session cookie gets set on `localhost:8080`
- The redirect goes to `http://localhost:8080/` (since `FRONTEND_URL` is empty)

Two fixes needed:

1. **`FRONTEND_URL` must default to `http://localhost:3001`** in `run-auth` when using the separate frontend dev server
2. **`callbackURL` should use `X-Forwarded-Host`** or the `FRONTEND_URL` so GitHub's callback goes through the frontend proxy (so the session cookie is on the right domain)

Actually, the callback URL issue is more fundamental. The GitHub OAuth callback must go to a URL that sets the cookie on the correct domain. If the frontend is on `localhost:3001`, the callback must go to `localhost:3001/auth/github/callback` so the session cookie is set on port 3001.

Let me fix this properly:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_bdrk_01ViyVgd8VTUbUzLxPondPrk
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/auth/oauth.go",
  "old_string": "func callbackURL(r *http.Request) string {\n\tscheme := \"https\"\n\tif r.TLS == nil {\n\t\tscheme = \"http\"\n\t}\n\tif fwd := r.Header.Get(\"X-Forwarded-Proto\"); fwd != \"\" {\n\t\tscheme = fwd\n\t}\n\treturn scheme + \"://\" + r.Host + \"/auth/github/callback\"\n}",
  "new_string": "func callbackURL(r *http.Request, frontendURL string) string {\n\t// When a frontend URL is configured (dev with separate frontend port),\n\t// use it so the GitHub callback goes through the frontend proxy and the\n\t// session cookie is set on the correct origin.\n\tif frontendURL != \"\" {\n\t\treturn strings.TrimRight(frontendURL, \"/\") + \"/auth/github/callback\"\n\t}\n\tscheme := \"https\"\n\tif r.TLS == nil {\n\t\tscheme = \"http\"\n\t}\n\tif fwd := r.Header.Get(\"X-Forwarded-Proto\"); fwd != \"\" {\n\t\tscheme = fwd\n\t}\n\treturn scheme + \"://\" + r.Host + \"/auth/github/callback\"\n}"
}
```

```
http://localhost:3001/auth/github/callback
```

```bash
REDACTED \

```

The flow should now be:
1. Click "Sign in with GitHub" → browser goes to `localhost:3001/auth/github`
2. Next.js proxies to backend → backend redirects to GitHub with callback URL `http://localhost:3001/auth/github/callback`
3. GitHub redirects back to `localhost:3001/auth/github/callback`
4. Next.js proxies to backend → session cookie set on `localhost:3001` → redirects to `http://localhost:3001/`

> DEVELOPER

The production never needs the auth is it because we don't set the github org right?

> AGENT

Right now production won't enforce auth if `NO_AUTH=true` is set, or if the GitHub OAuth env vars aren't configured.

Looking at the startup logic in `cmd/server/main.go:40-80`:

```go
noAuth := os.Getenv("NO_AUTH") == "true"
```

If `NO_AUTH` is not `"true"`, the server requires:
- `ALLOWED_GITHUB_USERS` or `ALLOWED_GITHUB_ORGS` (at least one)
- `SESSION_SECRET`
- `GITHUB_CLIENT_ID` and `GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET`

If any of those are missing, the server exits with an error.

So for production with auth enabled, you'd need to:
1. **Not** set `NO_AUTH=true`
2. Set `REDACTED` (or whatever org should have access)
3. Set `GITHUB_CLIENT_ID`, `GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET`, `SESSION_SECRET`

If you're currently running production with `NO_AUTH=true`, then yes — anyone can access it without logging in.
